  by capacities, with applications to regularity.
* **S. M. Nikol'skii, *Approximation of Functions of Several Variables
  and Imbedding Theorems***. The classical source for the
  approximation-theoretic definition of smoothness spaces.
* **O. V. Besov, V. P. Il'in, and S. M. Nikol'skii, *Integral
  Representations of Functions and Imbedding Theorems***. The large
  Russian reference behind much of the Besov scale.
* **M. A. Krasnosel'skii and Ya. B. Rutickii, *Convex Functions and
  Orlicz Spaces***. The classical source for Orlicz spaces.
* **M. M. Rao and Z. D. Ren, *Theory of Orlicz Spaces***. A
  comprehensive reference for Orlicz structure and integration.
* **Emmanuel Hebey, *Sobolev Spaces on Riemannian
  Manifolds***. Sobolev theory in geometric settings.
* **Haim Brezis, *Functional Analysis, Sobolev Spaces and Partial
  Differential Equations***. A concise route from Banach-space methods
  into Sobolev theory.
  Also listed in Section 15.
* **Hans Triebel, *The Structure of Functions***. Atomic, wavelet, and
  local-means characterizations of Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces, with
  fractal applications.
* **David E. Edmunds and W. Desmond Evans, *Hardy Operators, Function Spaces and
  Embeddings***. Hardy operators and the function-space embeddings they control. Distinct
  from their *Elliptic Differential Operators and Spectral Analysis*.

### More recent works

* **Giovanni Leoni, *A First Course in Fractional Sobolev Spaces***
  (2023, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/229), Graduate Studies in Mathematics).
  Fractional Sobolev spaces from the one-variable theory upward, with
  the fractional Laplacian as an application. The natural sequel to
  Leoni's *First Course in Sobolev Spaces*.
* **Claudia Bucur and Enrico Valdinoci, *Nonlocal Diffusion and
  Applications*** (2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-28739-3)). A short course on nonlocal elliptic
  equations and the fractional Laplacian.
  Also listed in Section 33.
* **Yoshihiro Sawano, *Theory of Besov Spaces*** (2018, Springer,
  Developments in Mathematics). A detailed treatment of Besov spaces, their
  characterisations, and related function spaces, complementing Triebel's
  monographs.
* **Wen Yuan, Winfried Sickel, and Dachun Yang, *Morrey and Campanato
  Meet Besov, Lizorkin and Triebel*** (2010, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-14606-0), Lecture Notes in
  Mathematics). Besov- and Triebel–Lizorkin-type spaces incorporating Morrey
  and Campanato parameters, with embeddings and characterisations.
* **Yoshihiro Sawano, Giuseppe Di Fazio, and Denny Ivanal Hakim, *Morrey
  Spaces: Introduction and Applications to Integral Operators and
  PDEs*** (2 volumes, 2020, Chapman and Hall/CRC; [I](https://doi.org/10.1201/9780429085925), [II](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781003029076)). A large modern
  treatment of Morrey spaces and their operators.
* **David R. Adams, *Morrey Spaces*** (2015,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-26681-7)). Morrey estimates,
  potentials, capacities, and applications to PDE. A concise research course
  beside the larger Morrey-space reference already listed.
* **Vakhtang Kokilashvili, Alexander Meskhi, Humberto Rafeiro, and
  Stefan Samko, *Integral Operators in Non-Standard Function Spaces***
  (2 volumes, 2016, Birkhäuser; [I](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-21015-5), [II](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-21018-6)). Singular integrals on Morrey, variable
  exponent, and grand Lebesgue spaces.
* **Luboš Pick, Alois Kufner, Oldřich John, and Svatopluk Fučík,
  *Function Spaces***, volume 1 (2nd ed., 2013, [De Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783110250428)), with later
  volumes. A systematic reference for Lebesgue, Orlicz, Lorentz, and
  rearrangement-invariant function spaces, including duality and embeddings.
* **Lars Diening, Petteri Harjulehto, Peter Hästö, and Michael
  Růžička, *Lebesgue and Sobolev Spaces with Variable
  Exponents*** (2011, Springer, Lecture Notes in Mathematics). The
  standard treatment of variable-exponent spaces.
* **David V. Cruz-Uribe and Alberto Fiorenza, *Variable Lebesgue Spaces:
  Foundations and Harmonic Analysis*** (2013,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-0348-0548-3)).
  Variable-exponent spaces, the maximal operator, and extrapolation. The
  companion to Diening–Harjulehto–Hästö–Růžička.
* **Petteri Harjulehto and Peter Hästö, *Orlicz Spaces and Generalized
  Orlicz Spaces*** (2019, Springer, Lecture Notes in Mathematics). The
  modern reference for Musielak–Orlicz settings and the associated
  harmonic analysis.
* **Juha Kinnunen, Juha Lehrbäck, and Antti Vähäkangas, *Maximal Function
  Methods for Sobolev Spaces*** (2021, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/257), Mathematical Surveys and
  Monographs). Pointwise inequalities, capacities, and Hardy inequalities
  for Sobolev functions through maximal functions, in Euclidean and metric
  settings.
* **Augusto C. Ponce, *Elliptic PDEs, Measures and Capacities: From the
  Poisson Equation to Nonlinear Thomas–Fermi Problems*** (2016,
  [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/140)). Sobolev capacities, measure data, and
  the borderline integrability of solutions.
  Also listed in Sections 33 and 38.
* **Haïm Brezis and Petru Mironescu, *Sobolev Maps to the Circle: From the
  Perspective of Analysis, Geometry, and Topology*** (2021,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-0716-1512-6)). Lifting, degree,
  and density questions for Sobolev and VMO maps with values in the circle.

---

<a id="ckma-27"></a>

## 27. Fourier Analysis and Real-Variable Harmonic Analysis

Fourier series and transforms, maximal functions, singular integrals,
weights, Littlewood–Paley theory, oscillatory integrals, and restriction are
studied on Euclidean and related real-variable spaces. Geometric,
time-frequency, and combinatorial methods are included where they serve
these problems. Section 28 treats groups and representations, Section 29
treats frames, wavelets, and sampling, and Section 30 treats microlocal
operator calculi. Real-variable Hardy spaces here complement the holomorphic
Hardy-space theory in Section 42.

### Foundational works

#### First and second courses

* **Elias M. Stein and Rami Shakarchi, *Fourier Analysis: An
  Introduction***. An introduction to Fourier series and transforms,
  connecting classical examples with applications to differential equations
  and analysis.
* **Yitzhak Katznelson, *An Introduction to Harmonic Analysis***. A compact
  treatment of Fourier series, Fourier transforms, and harmonic analysis on
  groups, with exercises and examples.
* **Elias M. Stein and Guido Weiss, *Introduction to Fourier Analysis
  on Euclidean Spaces***. Fourier analysis in Euclidean spaces, including
  Hardy spaces, conjugate harmonic functions, and spherical harmonics.
* **Javier Duoandikoetxea, *Fourier Analysis***. Short and excellent
  on real-variable methods, singular integrals, and weights.
* **Loukas Grafakos, *Classical Fourier Analysis*** and ***Modern
  Fourier Analysis***. Graduate treatments of maximal functions, singular
  integrals, multipliers, weighted estimates, Hardy spaces, Littlewood–Paley
  theory, and multilinear operators.
* **Mark A. Pinsky, *Introduction to Fourier Analysis and
  Wavelets***. An introductory treatment of Fourier series and transforms,
  with applications to differential equations and an introduction to
  wavelets.
* **T. W. Körner, *Fourier Analysis***. Idiosyncratic, classical, and
  full of the counterexamples that motivate the general theory.
* **Anton Deitmar and Siegfried Echterhoff, *Principles of Harmonic
  Analysis***. A modern unified treatment of the classical and
  abstract theories.
* **Henry Helson, *Harmonic Analysis***. A short, elegant course in
  Fourier series, the Fourier transform, and harmonic analysis on groups.

#### Research monographs and references

* **Elias M. Stein, *Singular Integrals and Differentiability
  Properties of Functions***. The foundational mature account of
  Calderón–Zygmund theory and its relation to Sobolev spaces.
* **Elias M. Stein, *Harmonic Analysis: Real-Variable Methods,
  Orthogonality, and Oscillatory Integrals***. An advanced treatment of
  maximal functions, oscillatory integrals, Fourier restriction, and
  real-variable methods in harmonic analysis.
* **Antoni Zygmund, *Trigonometric Series I–II***. A detailed classical
  reference on Fourier series, with particular attention to convergence,
  summability, and sharp one-dimensional results.
* **N. K. Bari, *A Treatise on Trigonometric Series***. The
  complementary Russian treatise, particularly on uniqueness,
  convergence, and basis questions.
* **Alberto Torchinsky, *Real-Variable Methods in Harmonic
  Analysis***. Hardy spaces, maximal functions, and singular integrals
  in a readable systematic account.
* **José García-Cuerva and José L. Rubio de Francia, *Weighted Norm
  Inequalities and Related Topics***. The fundamental monograph on
  Muckenhoupt weights, extrapolation, and vector-valued inequalities.
* **Christoph Thiele, *Wave Packet Analysis***. Wave packets and time-frequency
  analysis, including techniques for Carleson's theorem and the bilinear
  Hilbert transform.
* **Thomas H. Wolff, *Lectures on Harmonic Analysis***. Lectures on Fourier
  restriction, Kakeya problems, and geometric methods in harmonic analysis.
* **Christopher D. Sogge, *Fourier Integrals in Classical
  Analysis***. Oscillatory integrals, spectral clusters, and
  eigenfunction estimates.
* **Elias M. Stein, *Topics in Harmonic Analysis Related to the
  Littlewood–Paley Theory***. A short classic on square functions and
  semigroup methods.
* **Gerald B. Folland and Elias M. Stein, *Hardy Spaces on Homogeneous
  Groups***. Hardy spaces, singular integrals, and subelliptic analysis on
  homogeneous groups.
* **Alexander Nagel and Elias M. Stein, *Lectures on
  Pseudo-Differential Operators***, and their work on
  Carnot–Carathéodory metrics. The subelliptic strand of real-variable
  theory.
* **E. C. Titchmarsh, *Introduction to the Theory of Fourier
  Integrals***. A classical reference for the Fourier transform of a
  single variable.
  Also listed in Section 49.
* **Jean-Pierre Kahane, *Some Random Series of Functions***. Random
  Fourier series, and a model of how probability enters harmonic
  analysis.
* **Hugh L. Montgomery, *Ten Lectures on the Interface Between
  Analytic Number Theory and Harmonic Analysis***. Large-sieve inequalities,
  exponential sums, and Fourier-analytic estimates at the interface of
  harmonic analysis and analytic number theory.
* **Terence Tao and Van H. Vu, *Additive Combinatorics*** (2006,
  [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511755149); later corrected printings). Freiman's
  theorem, Szemerédi's theorem, and the Fourier-analytic arguments that
  became standard equipment.
* **Salomon Bochner, *Lectures on Fourier Integrals***. The classical
  lectures on Fourier integrals, positive-definite functions, and Bochner's
  theorem.
* **Salomon Bochner and Komaravolu Chandrasekharan, *Fourier
  Transforms***. A compact classical account of the L¹ and L²
  theory of Fourier transforms.
* **Robert E. Edwards and Garth I. Gaudry, *Littlewood–Paley and Multiplier
  Theory***. Littlewood–Paley theory and Fourier multipliers on groups.
* **Ronald R. Coifman and Guido Weiss, *Transference Methods in
  Analysis***. Transference of convolution operators and maximal functions
  between groups. The short CBMS account of a method used throughout
  real-variable and abstract harmonic analysis. Also listed in Section 28.
* **Helmut Groemer, *Geometric Applications of Fourier Series and Spherical
  Harmonics***. Fourier and spherical-harmonic methods for convex bodies,
  and the stability of geometric inequalities. Also listed in Section 14.
* **Frederick W. King, *Hilbert Transforms I–II***. The comprehensive
  reference on the Hilbert transform: properties, inequalities, and
  applications.
* **B. Golubov, A. Efimov, and V. Skvortsov, *Walsh Series and
  Transforms***. The standard reference on Walsh series, dyadic harmonic
  analysis, and its analogues of classical Fourier analysis.
* **John E. Gilbert and Margaret A. M. Murray, *Clifford Algebras and Dirac
  Operators in Harmonic Analysis***. Clifford algebras, Dirac operators, and
  their use in Euclidean harmonic analysis and representation theory.
* **Ralph P. Boas, Jr., *Integrability Theorems for Trigonometric Transforms***.
  Integrability of Fourier transforms of monotone functions and of wider classes. Distinct
  from his *Entire Functions*.

### More recent works

#### Courses

* **Loukas Grafakos, *Fundamentals of Fourier Analysis*** (2024,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-56500-7)). A graduate course in
  Fourier transforms, multipliers, and singular integrals, with extensive
  exercises. A separate, more classroom-oriented work than the author's
  *Classical* and *Modern Fourier Analysis*.

#### Real-variable theory: research monographs

* **David V. Cruz-Uribe, José Maria Martell, and Carlos Pérez, *Weights,
  Extrapolation and the Theory of Rubio de Francia*** (2011, Birkhäuser,
  Operator Theory: Advances and Applications). Weighted norm inequalities,
  Rubio de Francia extrapolation, and applications to operators in harmonic
  analysis.
* **Camil Muscalu and Wilhelm Schlag, *Classical and Multilinear
  Harmonic Analysis I–II*** (2013, Cambridge University Press, Cambridge
  Studies in Advanced Mathematics). The modern route to paraproducts,
  time-frequency methods, and multilinear estimates.
* **Vasily Vasyunin and Alexander Volberg, *The Bellman Function Technique in
  Harmonic Analysis*** (2020, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781108764469)). Sharp inequalities and
  harmonic-analysis estimates via Bellman functions. A systematic research
  treatment of a technique often learned from individual papers.
* **Brian Street, *Multi-parameter Singular Integrals*** (2014,
  [Princeton University Press](https://doi.org/10.1515/9781400852758), Annals of Mathematics Studies). The
  multi-parameter Calderón–Zygmund theory in the form developed with
  Stein.
* **Brian Street, *Maximal Subellipticity*** (2023, [De Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783111085647)). Listed in full in Section 33. Function spaces adapted to Carnot–Carathéodory
  geometries.
* **Ferenc Weisz, *Convergence and Summability of Fourier Transforms and Hardy
  Spaces*** (2017, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-56814-0)).
  Summability, maximal operators, and Hardy-space estimates for Fourier
  transforms. A specialist account of convergence questions beyond the
  elementary L2 theory.
* **Wilfredo Urbina-Romero, *Gaussian Harmonic Analysis*** (2019,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-05597-4)). Gaussian measure,
  Hermite expansions, the Ornstein–Uhlenbeck semigroup, and associated
  operators. The counterpart of Euclidean harmonic analysis for Gaussian
  spaces.
* **Pascal Auscher and Moritz Egert, *Boundary Value Problems and Hardy
  Spaces for Elliptic Systems with Block Structure*** (2023,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-29973-5)). Listed in full in Section 25. Hardy spaces adapted to operators.
* **Alexei Poltoratski, *Toeplitz Approach to Problems of the
  Uncertainty Principle*** (2015, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/cbms/121)). Listed in full in Section 21.
* **Tuomas Hytönen, Jan van Neerven, Mark Veraar, and Lutz Weis,
  *Analysis in Banach Spaces I–III*** (2016–2023, Springer, Ergebnisse der
  Mathematik und ihrer Grenzgebiete). The vector-valued theory.
  Also listed in Section 18.

#### Restriction, geometric, and additive-combinatorial methods

* **Ciprian Demeter, *Fourier Restriction, Decoupling, and
  Applications*** (2020, Cambridge University Press, Cambridge Studies in
  Advanced Mathematics). The standard book on decoupling and its arithmetic
  and PDE consequences.
* **Pertti Mattila, *Fourier Analysis and Hausdorff
  Dimension*** (2015, Cambridge University Press, Cambridge Studies in
  Advanced Mathematics). Projections, distance sets, and the
  Fourier-analytic side of fractal geometry.
  Also listed in Section 37.
* **Larry Guth, *Polynomial Methods in Combinatorics*** (2016, [American
  Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/ulect/064), University Lecture Series).
  Polynomial partitioning, the joints problem, and applications of incidence
  geometry to harmonic analysis.
* **Giancarlo Travaglini, *Number Theory, Fourier Analysis and Geometric
  Discrepancy*** (2014, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781107358379)). Fourier methods for
  lattice-point counts and discrepancy of geometric sets. Included for its
  harmonic-analysis tools, with number theory providing the applications.
* **Terence Tao, *Higher Order Fourier Analysis*** (2012, [American
  Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/142), Graduate Studies in Mathematics). Gowers norms,
  nilsequences, and the inverse theorems, written as analysis.
  Also listed in Section 10.

---

<a id="ckma-28"></a>

## 28. Abstract Harmonic Analysis on Groups

Haar measure, convolution, characters, unitary representations, and
Plancherel theory organise harmonic analysis on locally compact groups.
Abelian, compact, Lie, nilpotent, and symmetric-space settings are
represented, with related topics in positive definite functions, rigidity,
and random walks. Section 20 studies the associated Banach algebras, Section
23 their operator-algebraic counterparts, and Section 27 Euclidean harmonic
analysis. Representation-theoretic background is included to support the
analytic constructions and estimates.

### Foundational works

* **Gerald B. Folland, *A Course in Abstract Harmonic Analysis***. A graduate
  introduction to Haar measure, unitary representations, and the Plancherel
  theorem on locally compact groups.
* **Ronald R. Coifman and Guido Weiss, *Transference Methods in
  Analysis***. Listed in full in Section 27.
* **Edwin Hewitt and Kenneth A. Ross, *Abstract Harmonic Analysis
  I–II***. A two-volume reference on locally compact groups, Haar measure,
  representation theory, and duality.
  Also listed in Section 1.
* **Walter Rudin, *Fourier Analysis on Groups***. A compact classic on
  locally compact abelian groups, idempotent measures, Sidon sets, and
  Fourier–Stieltjes theory.
* **Hans Reiter and Jan D. Stegeman, *Classical Harmonic Analysis and
  Locally Compact Groups***. A systematic modern account with careful
  treatment of the Segal-algebra viewpoint.
* **Lynn H. Loomis, *An Introduction to Abstract Harmonic
  Analysis***. Short, classical, and algebra-first.
  Also listed in Section 20.
* **Jacques Faraut, *Analysis on Lie Groups: An Introduction*** (2008,
  [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511755170)).
  Matrix Lie groups, Haar measure, the Peter–Weyl theorem, and spherical
  harmonics, as a first course in harmonic analysis on groups.
* **Brian C. Hall, *Lie Groups, Lie Algebras, and Representations: An
  Elementary Introduction*** (2nd ed., 2015, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-13467-3), Graduate Texts
  in Mathematics; first edition 2003). The usual first book on compact groups and their
  representations for analysts.
* **Daniel Bump, *Lie Groups*** (2nd ed., 2013, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-8024-2), Graduate Texts
  in Mathematics; first edition 2004). A graduate treatment of Lie groups
  and their finite- and infinite-dimensional representations, with
  applications to harmonic analysis.
* **Sigurður Helgason, *Groups and Geometric Analysis*** and
  ***Geometric Analysis on Symmetric Spaces***. Spherical functions,
  invariant differential operators, Radon transforms, and analysis on
  symmetric spaces.
* **Anthony W. Knapp, *Representation Theory of Semisimple Groups: An
  Overview Based on Examples***. An introduction to representations of
  semisimple groups through examples, with the analytic background needed
  for harmonic analysis.
* **Anthony W. Knapp, *Lie Groups Beyond an Introduction***. The
  structural background that harmonic analysis on groups assumes.
* **Nolan R. Wallach, *Real Reductive Groups I–II***. The
  research-level treatment of harmonic analysis on reductive groups.
* **V. S. Varadarajan, *An Introduction to Harmonic Analysis on
  Semisimple Lie Groups***. A concise bridge into the noncommutative
  theory.
* **Roe Goodman and Nolan R. Wallach, *Symmetry, Representations, and
  Invariants*** (2009, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-387-79852-3), Graduate Texts in Mathematics). A
  large course in the invariant theory and representation theory used
  in harmonic analysis.
* **Audrey Terras, *Harmonic Analysis on Symmetric Spaces*** (2 volumes, 2nd
  ed., 2013–2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-7972-7); [volume II](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4939-3408-9); first edition 1985–1988).
  Fourier analysis on Euclidean space, the sphere, the upper half-plane, and
  spaces of positive matrices, with applications to number theory and
  physics.
* **I. M. Gel'fand, M. I. Graev, and N. Ya. Vilenkin, *Generalized
  Functions*, volume 5**. Integral geometry and representation theory
  in the Gelfand style.
  Also listed in Section 16.
* **Gerald B. Folland, *Harmonic Analysis in Phase Space***. The
  Heisenberg group, Weyl calculus, and the metaplectic representation.
  Also listed in Section 30.
* **Michael E. Taylor, *Noncommutative Harmonic Analysis***. Analysis
  on Lie groups oriented toward PDE and spectral theory.
* **Mitsuo Sugiura, *Unitary Representations and Harmonic
  Analysis***. A readable route into the concrete examples.
* **Alain Robert, *Introduction to the Representation Theory of
  Compact and Locally Compact Groups***. Short and useful for the
  compact case.
* **A. A. Kirillov, *Lectures on the Orbit Method*** (2004, [American
  Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/064), Graduate Studies
  in Mathematics). Coadjoint orbits as a guide to unitary representations,
  by the founder of the method.
* **M. Bachir Bekka, Pierre de la Harpe, and Alain Valette, *Kazhdan's
  Property (T)*** (2008, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511542749)). The standard
  monograph on property (T).
* **Ramesh Gangolli and V. S. Varadarajan, *Harmonic Analysis of Spherical
  Functions on Real Reductive Groups***. Harish-Chandra's theory of
  spherical functions and the Plancherel formula.
* **Garth Warner, *Harmonic Analysis on Semi-Simple Lie Groups
  I–II***. A detailed account of Harish-Chandra's work.
* **Gerrit Heckman and Henrik Schlichtkrull, *Harmonic Analysis and
  Special Functions on Symmetric Spaces***. Hypergeometric functions
  associated with root systems and harmonic analysis on symmetric spaces.
* **Sundaram Thangavelu, *Harmonic Analysis on the Heisenberg
  Group***. The Weyl transform, Hermite and Laguerre expansions, and
  restriction theorems on the Heisenberg group.
* **Colin C. Graham and O. Carruth McGehee, *Essays in Commutative Harmonic
  Analysis***. Thin sets, Helson sets, and the structure of measure
  algebras on locally compact abelian groups.
* **Herbert Heyer, *Probability Measures on Locally Compact
  Groups***. Convolution semigroups, Lévy–Khintchine formulas, and
  central limit theorems on groups.
* **Peter D. Lax and Ralph S. Phillips, *Scattering Theory for
  Automorphic Functions***. The Lax–Phillips scattering framework applied
  to the spectral theory of the modular group.
* **Christian Berg, Jens Peter Reus Christensen, and Paul Ressel, *Harmonic Analysis on
  Semigroups: Theory of Positive Definite and Related Functions***. Positive definite
  functions on semigroups and the harmonic analysis they carry.
* **Christian Berg and Gunnar Forst, *Potential Theory on Locally Compact Abelian Groups***.
  Listed in full in Section 38. Convolution semigroups and potential kernels on abelian
  groups.
* **Cho-Ho Chu and Anthony To-Ming Lau, *Harmonic Functions on Groups and Fourier
  Algebras***. Harmonic functions on locally compact groups and their relation to the
  Fourier algebra.
* **Yurii I. Lyubich, *Introduction to the Theory of Banach Representations of Groups***.
  Representations of groups on Banach spaces and the spectral theory of the operators they
  generate.
* **Valery V. Volchkov and Vitaly V. Volchkov, *Harmonic Analysis of Mean Periodic Functions
  on Symmetric Spaces and the Heisenberg Group***. Mean-periodic functions, spectral
  synthesis, and the Pompeiu problem on symmetric spaces and the Heisenberg group. Distinct
  from their *Offbeat Integral Geometry on Symmetric Spaces*.

### More recent works

* **Emmanuel Kowalski, *An Introduction to the Representation Theory of
  Groups*** (2014, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/155), Graduate Studies in
  Mathematics). A graduate course in representations with harmonic
  analysis in view.
* **M. Bachir Bekka and Pierre de la Harpe, *Unitary Representations of Groups,
  Duals, and Characters*** (2020, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://www.ams.org/books/surv/250/)). Unitary duals, characters,
  and representation theory of locally compact and discrete groups. A research
  reference connecting abstract harmonic analysis with operator algebras.
* **Eberhard Kaniuth and Anthony To-Ming Lau, *Fourier and
  Fourier–Stieltjes Algebras on Locally Compact Groups*** (2018, American
  Mathematical Society, Mathematical Surveys and Monographs). The
  algebra-theoretic modern reference.
  Also listed in Section 20.
* **Eberhard Kaniuth and Keith F. Taylor, *Induced Representations of
  Locally Compact Groups*** (2013, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139045391)). Mackey's induction
  theory, the imprimitivity theorem, and the topology of duals.
* **Antoine Derighetti, *Convolution Operators on Groups*** (2011,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-20656-6), Lecture Notes of the Unione Matematica Italiana). Convolution operators,
  Figà-Talamanca–Herz algebras, and amenability.
* **Kate Juschenko, *Amenability of Discrete Groups by Examples*** (2022,
  [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/266),
  Mathematical Surveys and Monographs). Criteria for amenability and the
  recent examples, including topological full groups and extensive
  amenability.
* **Véronique Fischer and Michael Ruzhansky, *Quantization on Nilpotent
  Lie Groups*** (2016, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-29558-9), Progress in Mathematics).
  Global quantization on graded nilpotent Lie groups, with background on
  homogeneous groups, Rockland operators, and their Sobolev spaces.
  Also listed in Section 30.
* **Michael Ruzhansky and Ville Turunen, *Pseudo-Differential Operators
  and Symmetries: Background Analysis and Advanced Topics*** (2010,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-7643-8514-9)). Global pseudo-differential operators adapted to
  symmetries and groups.
  Also listed in Section 30.
* **Michael Ruzhansky and Durvudkhan Suragan, *Hardy Inequalities on
  Homogeneous Groups*** (2019, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-02895-4)). Listed in full in Section 11.
* **Hidenori Fujiwara and Jean Ludwig, *Harmonic Analysis on Exponential
  Solvable Lie Groups*** (2015,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-4-431-55288-8)). Representation
  theory, the orbit method, and harmonic analysis on exponential solvable
  groups. A research monograph for a class not covered in detail by the
  compact and semisimple texts.
* **Palle Jorgensen, Steen Pedersen, and Feng Tian, *Extensions of Positive
  Definite Functions: Applications and Their Harmonic Analysis*** (2016,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-39780-1)). Positive definite
  kernels, extension problems, and unitary representations. A specialised
  treatment connecting harmonic analysis with reproducing kernels and spectral
  measures.
* **Sigurdur Helgason, *Integral Geometry and Radon Transforms*** (2011,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4419-6055-9)). Radon transforms
  on Euclidean and symmetric spaces, support theorems, and inversion
  formulas.
* **Valery V. Volchkov and Vitaly V. Volchkov, *Offbeat Integral Geometry on
  Symmetric Spaces*** (2013,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-0348-0572-8)). Integral
  transforms, support and uniqueness questions, and mean-value problems on
  symmetric spaces. A research continuation of the classical Radon-transform
  theory.
* **Tullio Ceccherini-Silberstein, Fabio Scarabotti, and Filippo Tolli,
  *Discrete Harmonic Analysis: Representations, Number Theory,
  Expanders, and the Fourier Transform*** (2018, [Cambridge University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781316856383)). Finite and discrete harmonic analysis in a form useful beyond
  group theory. Their *Harmonic Analysis on Finite Groups* (2008) and
  *Representation Theory and Harmonic Analysis of Wreath Products of
  Finite Groups* (2014), both Cambridge, are the more specialised
  companions.
* **Yves Benoist and Jean-François Quint, *Random Walks on Reductive
  Groups*** (2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-47721-3)). Listed in full in Section 10.
* **David Applebaum, *Probability on Compact Lie Groups*** (2014,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-07842-7)). Listed in full in Section 8.

---

<a id="ckma-29"></a>

## 29. Wavelets, Frames, Time–Frequency Analysis, and Sampling

Wavelets, multiresolution analysis, frames, Gabor systems, time–frequency
representations, and sampling describe functions through structured
expansions and measurements. Modulation spaces, sparse recovery, and
directional systems are included through their analytic foundations and
approximation properties. Section 27 provides Fourier and harmonic-analysis
methods, Section 26 provides smoothness spaces, and Section 47 treats
approximation more generally. The emphasis is on representation, stability,
localization, and reconstruction rather than on signal-processing
applications alone.

### Foundational works

* **Ingrid Daubechies, *Ten Lectures on Wavelets***. The foundational
  graduate monograph on orthonormal wavelets and multiresolution
  analysis.
* **Yves Meyer, *Wavelets and Operators*** (with the second volume by
  Meyer and Coifman, *Calderón–Zygmund and Multilinear
  Operators*). Wavelets as an instrument of singular-integral theory.
* **Eugenio Hernández and Guido Weiss, *A First Course on
  Wavelets***. A graduate course on Fourier-analytic constructions of
  wavelets, multiresolution analysis, and their basic properties.
* **Przemysław Wojtaszczyk, *A Mathematical Introduction to
  Wavelets***. Wavelets from the function-space and approximation
  viewpoint.
* **Stéphane Mallat, *A Wavelet Tour of Signal Processing***. Broader
  and more computational, but mathematically substantial and still an
  important reference.
* **Albert Cohen, *Numerical Analysis of Wavelet
  Methods***. Approximation spaces, nonlinear approximation, and the
  link to Besov scales.
* **Karlheinz Gröchenig, *Foundations of Time-Frequency
  Analysis***. The standard mathematical monograph on Gabor analysis,
  modulation spaces, and frames.
* **Ole Christensen, *An Introduction to Frames and Riesz
  Bases***. The functional-analytic foundation of frame theory.
* **Robert M. Young, *An Introduction to Nonharmonic Fourier
  Series***. The classical account of Riesz bases of exponentials and
  completeness problems.
* **Kristian Seip, *Interpolation and Sampling in Spaces of Analytic
  Functions***. Sampling and interpolation via complex analysis and
  density conditions.
* **Hans G. Feichtinger and Thomas Strohmer (eds.), *Gabor Analysis
  and Algorithms*** and ***Advances in Gabor Analysis***. Collections
  that function as references for the subject.
* **M. W. Wong, *Wavelet Transforms and Localization Operators***. Localization operators
  defined through the wavelet transform. Distinct from R. Wong, *Asymptotic Approximations
  of Integrals*.

### More recent works

* **Christopher Heil, *A Basis Theory Primer*** (expanded ed., 2011,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-8176-4687-5)). Bases, frames, and the functional analysis behind
  time-frequency expansions.
* **Árpád Bényi and Kasso A. Okoudjou, *Modulation Spaces: With
  Applications to Pseudodifferential Operators and Nonlinear
  Schrödinger Equations*** (2020, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-0716-0332-1)). The graduate monograph
  on modulation spaces after Gröchenig.
* **Elena Cordero and Luigi Rodino, *Time-Frequency Analysis of
  Operators*** (2020, [De Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783110532456)). Pseudodifferential and Fourier
  integral operators in the time-frequency calculus.
* **Maurice A. de Gosson, *Symplectic Methods in Harmonic Analysis and
  in Mathematical Physics*** (2011, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-7643-9992-4)). The Weyl calculus,
  the Wigner distribution, and symplectic covariance.
* **Peter G. Casazza and Gitta Kutyniok (eds.), *Finite Frames: Theory
  and Applications*** (2013, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-8176-8373-3)). A reference volume for finite
  frame theory.
* **Shayne F. D. Waldron, *An Introduction to Finite Tight Frames*** (2018,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-8176-4815-2)). Finite tight
  frames, equiangular lines, and frames arising from group representations.
* **Bin Han, *Framelets and Wavelets: Algorithms, Analysis, and
  Applications*** (2017, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-68530-4)).
  A research monograph on wavelets and framelets, connecting their analytic
  properties with construction methods and algorithms.
* **Aleksandr Krivoshein, Vladimir Protasov, and Maria Skopina, *Multivariate
  Wavelet Frames*** (2016,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-981-10-3205-9)). Refinement
  equations, matrix masks, and construction of multivariate wavelet frames. A
  specialised reference beyond the one-dimensional wavelet theory.
* **Gitta Kutyniok and Demetrio Labate (eds.), *Shearlets: Multiscale Analysis
  for Multivariate Data*** (2012,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-8176-8316-0)). Directional
  multiscale systems, sparse approximation, and geometric singularities. A
  collection of lecture-style treatments extending the wavelet framework to
  anisotropic features.
* **Alexander Olevskii and Alexander Ulanovskii, *Functions with
  Disconnected Spectrum: Sampling, Interpolation, Translates*** (2016,
  American Mathematical Society, University Lecture Series). Sampling and
  interpolation for functions with spectrum in a given set.
* **Simon Foucart and Holger Rauhut, *A Mathematical Introduction to
  Compressive Sensing*** (2013, Birkhäuser, Applied and Numerical Harmonic
  Analysis). Sparse recovery and compressed sensing, using concentration
  inequalities, random matrices, and approximation theory.
* **Andrei Osipov, Vladimir Rokhlin, and Hong Xiao, *Prolate Spheroidal Wave Functions of
  Order Zero: Mathematical Tools for Bandlimited Approximation*** (2013,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-8259-8)). Prolate spheroidal wave functions
  and their use in bandlimited approximation.

---

<a id="ckma-30"></a>

## 30. Distributions, Pseudodifferential Operators, Microlocal and Semiclassical Analysis

Distributions, pseudodifferential and Fourier integral operators,
phase-space methods, and semiclassical analysis describe singularities,
propagation, quantization, and spectral asymptotics. Resonances and
hypoelliptic estimates are included where these calculi are central. Section
16 develops the topological foundations of distributions, Section 27 the
underlying harmonic analysis, and Section 32 general linear PDE. Sections 22
and 53 treat operator-theoretic and geometric spectral questions; Section 50
treats asymptotic methods more broadly.

### Foundational works

* **Lars Hörmander, *The Analysis of Linear Partial Differential
  Operators I***. The working reference for distributions, Fourier
  transforms, wavefront sets, and constant-coefficient equations.
* **Lars Hörmander, *The Analysis of Linear Partial Differential
  Operators II–IV***. Differential operators with variable
  coefficients, pseudodifferential operators, propagation of
  singularities, and Fourier integral operators.
* **Lars Hörmander, *Linear Partial Differential Operators***. The
  earlier, shorter route into the constant-coefficient and
  distributional theory.
* **François Trèves, *Introduction to Pseudodifferential and Fourier
  Integral Operators I–II***. A large systematic presentation of
  symbolic and Fourier-integral methods.
* **Michael E. Taylor, *Pseudodifferential Operators***. A concise
  geometric-analytic treatment.
* **Michael E. Taylor, *Pseudodifferential Operators and Nonlinear
  PDE***. Paradifferential calculus and microlocal methods for
  nonlinear equations.
* **Guy Métivier, *Para-Differential Calculus and Applications to the
  Cauchy Problem for Nonlinear Systems*** (2008, [Edizioni della
  Normale](https://edizioni.sns.it/prodotto/para-differential-calculus-and-applications-to-the-cauchy-problem-for-nonlinear-systems/)). Paradifferential calculus in the form used for nonlinear
  hyperbolic equations.
* **Alain Grigis and Johannes Sjöstrand, *Microlocal Analysis for
  Differential Operators***. A concise introduction to pseudodifferential
  operators, Fourier integral operators, and the propagation of
  singularities.
* **Johannes J. Duistermaat, *Fourier Integral Operators***. The classic
  geometric treatment of canonical relations and oscillatory
  integrals.
* **M. A. Shubin, *Pseudodifferential Operators and Spectral
  Theory***. Global pseudodifferential analysis and spectral
  asymptotics.
  Also listed in Section 53.
* **Xavier Saint Raymond, *Elementary Introduction to the Theory of
  Pseudodifferential Operators***. An accessible short entry point.
* **Serge Alinhac and Patrick Gérard, *Pseudo-differential Operators and the
  Nash–Moser Theorem*** (2007, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/082), Graduate Studies in
  Mathematics). Pseudodifferential and paradifferential calculus, followed
  by the Nash–Moser theorem and its applications. Short and well chosen.
* **Mouez Dimassi and Johannes Sjöstrand, *Spectral Asymptotics in the
  Semi-Classical Limit***. Semiclassical pseudodifferential methods and
  spectral asymptotics, including trace formulas and estimates for
  Schrödinger operators.
  Also listed in Section 50.
* **André Martinez, *An Introduction to Semiclassical and Microlocal
  Analysis***. An introduction to semiclassical and microlocal methods,
  including pseudodifferential operators, WKB constructions, and spectral
  applications.
* **Gerald B. Folland, *Harmonic Analysis in Phase Space***. Weyl
  quantization and the Heisenberg-group foundation.
  Also listed in Section 28.
* **Victor Guillemin and Shlomo Sternberg, *Geometric Asymptotics***. The
  symplectic side of oscillatory integrals and spectral asymptotics. Their
  later *Semi-Classical Analysis* is listed among the more recent works.
* **Richard B. Melrose, *The Atiyah–Patodi–Singer Index Theorem*** and
  ***Geometric Scattering Theory***. Microlocal analysis on manifolds
  with boundary and at infinity.
  Also listed in Section 53.
* **Frédéric Pham, *Singularities of Integrals: Homology, Hyperfunctions and
  Microlocal Analysis*** (2011; English translation of the 2005 French work,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-85729-603-0)). Integration cycles,
  singularities, and microlocal ideas developed through geometry and topology.
  Useful background for asymptotic analysis and analytic continuation of
  parameter-dependent integrals.
* **Bernard Helffer and Francis Nier, *Hypoelliptic Estimates and Spectral
  Theory for Fokker–Planck Operators and Witten Laplacians*** (2005,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/b104762), Lecture Notes in
  Mathematics). Hypoellipticity and return to equilibrium for kinetic
  Fokker–Planck operators, and semiclassical analysis of Witten Laplacians.
* **Cédric Villani, *Hypocoercivity*** (2009, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/S0065-9266-09-00567-5), Memoirs). The
  abstract framework for exponential convergence to equilibrium of
  degenerate dissipative equations such as the kinetic Fokker–Planck
  equation.
* **Masaki Kashiwara, Takahiro Kawai, and Tatsuo Kimura, *Foundations of
  Algebraic Analysis***. Hyperfunctions, microfunctions, and
  microlocal analysis in the Sato school's approach.
* **V. P. Maslov and M. V. Fedoriuk, *Semi-Classical Approximation in
  Quantum Mechanics***. The canonical operator and Maslov index; the
  founding work on global WKB approximation.
* **Yuri V. Egorov and Bert-Wolfgang Schulze, *Pseudo-Differential Operators, Singularities,
  Applications***. Pseudodifferential operators on manifolds with singularities, and the
  edge calculus.
* **B. A. Plamenevskii, *Algebras of Pseudodifferential Operators***. Algebras of
  pseudodifferential operators, including operators with discontinuous symbols.

### More recent works

* **Maciej Zworski, *Semiclassical Analysis*** (2012, American
  Mathematical Society). The standard graduate text for semiclassical
  calculus, microlocalization, and applications to spectral theory and
  resonances.
* **Victor Guillemin and Shlomo Sternberg, *Semi-Classical Analysis***
  (2013, International Press). Semiclassical analysis from the symplectic
  viewpoint: WKB, Fourier integral operators, the Maslov index, and
  spectral asymptotics.
* **Nicolas Lerner, *Metrics on the Phase Space and Non-Selfadjoint
  Pseudo-Differential Operators*** (2010, Birkhäuser). The modern
  Weyl–Hörmander calculus, done thoroughly.
* **Hajer Bahouri, Jean-Yves Chemin, and Raphaël Danchin, *Fourier
  Analysis and Nonlinear Partial Differential Equations*** (2011,
  Springer). Paradifferential calculus and Littlewood–Paley methods for
  nonlinear PDE. Also listed in Section 34.
* **Fabio Nicola and Luigi Rodino, *Global Pseudo-Differential Calculus on
  Euclidean Spaces*** (2010,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-7643-8512-5)). Shubin and SG
  symbol classes, global ellipticity, and spectral asymptotics on R^n.
* **Semyon Dyatlov and Maciej Zworski, *Mathematical Theory of
  Scattering Resonances*** (2019, American Mathematical Society). Scattering
  resonances for differential operators, developed through spectral,
  complex-analytic, and microlocal methods.
* **Victor Ivrii, *Microlocal Analysis, Sharp Spectral Asymptotics and
  Applications*** (5 volumes, 2019, Springer; [I](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-30557-4), [II](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-30541-3), [III](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-30537-6), [IV](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-30545-1), [V](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-30561-1)). The expanded treatise on
  sharp Weyl laws, propagation, and applications to quantum theory.
  It extends Ivrii's 1998 *Microlocal Analysis and Precise Spectral
  Asymptotics*, listed in Sections 50 and 53.
  Also listed in Sections 50 and 53.
* **Nicolas Lerner, *Carleman Inequalities: An Introduction and More*** (2019,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-15993-1)). A systematic
  treatment of weighted estimates, unique continuation, and applications. A
  distinct later monograph beside Lerner's phase-space book listed above.
* **Alberto Parmeggiani, *Spectral Theory of Non-commutative Harmonic
  Oscillators: An Introduction*** (2010, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-11922-4), Lecture Notes in
  Mathematics). A short research introduction to a model non-commuting
  system.
* **Véronique Fischer and Michael Ruzhansky, *Quantization on Nilpotent
  Lie Groups*** (2016, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-29558-9)). Listed in full in Section 28.
* **Michael Ruzhansky and Ville Turunen, *Pseudo-Differential Operators
  and Symmetries*** (2010, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-7643-8514-9)). Listed in full in Section 28.
* **Brian Street, *Maximal Subellipticity*** (2023, [De Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783111085647)). Listed in full in Section 33.
* **Monique Combescure and Didier Robert, *Coherent States and
  Applications in Mathematical Physics*** (2nd ed., 2021, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-70845-0)).
  Listed in full in Section 22.
* **Gabriel P. Paternain, Mikko Salo, and Gunther Uhlmann, *Geometric
  Inverse Problems*** (2023, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781009039901)). Listed in full in Section 22. Microlocal analysis applied to integral geometry.

# Part VI. Differential Equations, Variational Methods, and Geometric Measure Theory

---

<a id="ckma-31"></a>

## 31. Ordinary Differential Equations and Dynamical Systems

Existence and uniqueness for ordinary differential equations lead to
stability, invariant manifolds, bifurcation, Hamiltonian dynamics, and
chaotic behaviour. Complex-domain equations, functional differential
equations, and dynamics of dissipative evolution are included where the same
qualitative and analytic methods apply. Section 10 emphasises invariant
measures and ergodic structure, Section 35 abstract nonlinear existence and
bifurcation, Section 46 holomorphic iteration, and Section 51 integrable
systems. Perturbation and averaging also connect with Section 50.

### Foundational works

* **Earl A. Coddington and Norman Levinson, *Theory of Ordinary
  Differential Equations***. The classical graduate reference for
  existence, linear systems, asymptotics, and boundary problems.
* **Philip Hartman, *Ordinary Differential Equations***. A
  foundational qualitative treatment of flows, stability, invariant
  manifolds, and asymptotic behaviour.
* **Jack K. Hale, *Ordinary Differential Equations***. Dynamical and
  stability methods, especially influential for the qualitative
  theory.
* **Vladimir I. Arnold, *Ordinary Differential Equations*** and
  ***Geometrical Methods in the Theory of Ordinary Differential
  Equations***. Short, geometric, and conceptually decisive.
* **Carmen Chicone, *Ordinary Differential Equations with
  Applications***. Strong on qualitative methods, averaging, and
  perturbation.
* **Lawrence Perko, *Differential Equations and Dynamical
  Systems***. A standard source for planar and finite-dimensional
  dynamics.
* **Morris W. Hirsch, Stephen Smale, and Robert L. Devaney,
  *Differential Equations, Dynamical Systems, and an Introduction to
  Chaos***. A geometric first graduate introduction.
* **James D. Meiss, *Differential Dynamical Systems*** (revised ed.,
  2017, [SIAM](https://doi.org/10.1137/1.9781611974645)). A geometric graduate course in finite-dimensional
  dynamics, with careful treatment of invariant manifolds.
* **Jack K. Hale and Hüseyin Koçak, *Dynamics and Bifurcations***. A
  clear route into bifurcation theory.
* **Yuri A. Kuznetsov, *Elements of Applied Bifurcation Theory***. The
  standard systematic reference for normal forms and bifurcations.
* **Stephen Wiggins, *Introduction to Applied Nonlinear Dynamical
  Systems and Chaos***. Broad coverage of invariant manifolds,
  perturbation, and chaotic dynamics.
* **John Guckenheimer and Philip Holmes, *Nonlinear Oscillations,
  Dynamical Systems, and Bifurcations of Vector Fields***. A graduate
  treatment of bifurcations, normal forms, periodic orbits, homoclinic
  phenomena, and chaotic dynamics.
* **Jan A. Sanders, Ferdinand Verhulst, and James Murdock, *Averaging
  Methods in Nonlinear Dynamical Systems*** (2nd ed., 2007,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-387-48918-6), Applied
  Mathematical Sciences). Averaging with error estimates, normal forms, and
  passage through resonance. Also listed in Section 50.
* **Anatole Katok and Boris Hasselblatt, *Introduction to the Modern
  Theory of Dynamical Systems***. The standard large graduate
  reference for smooth and hyperbolic dynamics.
  Also listed in Section 10.
* **Jacob Palis and Welington de Melo, *Geometric Theory of Dynamical
  Systems: An Introduction***. Structural stability, genericity, and the
  Kupka–Smale theorem, in the geometric theory of flows and
  diffeomorphisms.
* **Clark Robinson, *Dynamical Systems: Stability, Symbolic Dynamics,
  and Chaos***. A thorough alternative with careful proofs.
* **Christian Bonatti, Lorenzo J. Díaz, and Marcelo Viana, *Dynamics
  Beyond Uniform Hyperbolicity*** (2005, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/b138174)). Listed in full in Section 10.
* **Jürgen Moser and Eduard J. Zehnder, *Notes on Dynamical Systems***
  (2005, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/cln/012),
  Courant Lecture Notes). Hamiltonian systems, KAM theory, and periodic
  orbits, from Moser's lectures.
* **Vladimir I. Arnold, Valery V. Kozlov, and Anatoly I. Neishtadt,
  *Mathematical Aspects of Classical and Celestial Mechanics*** (3rd ed.,
  2006, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-48926-9)).
  Integrability, perturbation theory, averaging, and KAM theory, surveyed
  with the precision of a reference.
* **Heinz Hanßmann, *Local and Semi-Local Bifurcations in Hamiltonian
  Dynamical Systems: Results and Examples*** (2007, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/3-540-38894-X), Lecture
  Notes in Mathematics). Bifurcations that preserve a Hamiltonian
  structure.
* **Jack K. Hale, *Asymptotic Behavior of Dissipative
  Systems***. Attractors and long-time behaviour of dissipative
  evolution equations.
* **Roger Temam, *Infinite-Dimensional Dynamical Systems in Mechanics
  and Physics***. Attractors for the evolution equations of mechanics
  and physics.
* **James C. Robinson, *Infinite-Dimensional Dynamical Systems: An
  Introduction to Dissipative Parabolic PDEs and the Theory of Global
  Attractors*** (2001, [Cambridge University Press](https://www.cambridgebookshop.co.uk/products/infinite-dimensional-dynamical-systems)). Global attractors, their
  dimension, and inertial manifolds, with the Navier–Stokes equations as the
  main example.
* **Vladimir V. Chepyzhov and Mark I. Vishik, *Attractors for Equations of
  Mathematical Physics*** (2002, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/coll/049), Colloquium Publications).
  Global and trajectory attractors, including nonautonomous problems and
  equations without uniqueness.
* **Sergei B. Kuksin, *Analysis of Hamiltonian PDEs*** (2000, [Oxford
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1093/oso/9780198503958.001.0001)).
  KAM theory for Hamiltonian PDEs and their long-time behaviour.
* **Massimiliano Berti, *Nonlinear Oscillations of Hamiltonian PDEs***
  (2007, [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-8176-4681-3)). Periodic
  solutions of nonlinear wave equations by variational methods and
  Nash–Moser iteration, small divisors included.
* **Wolfgang Wasow, *Asymptotic Expansions for Ordinary Differential
  Equations***. The classical reference for singular points and
  asymptotic ODE theory. Also listed in Section 50.
* **Earl A. Coddington and Robert Carlson, *Linear Ordinary
  Differential Equations***. Linear ordinary differential equations
  and their spectral theory in modern form.
* **Anton Zettl, *Sturm–Liouville Theory***. The modern systematic
  account of the Sturm–Liouville problem.
  Also listed in Section 22.
* **Yulij Ilyashenko and Sergei Yakovenko, *Lectures on Analytic
  Differential Equations***. The complex-analytic theory: monodromy,
  normal forms, and limit cycles.
* **Einar Hille, *Ordinary Differential Equations in the Complex
  Domain***. The classical treatment of the analytic theory.
* **V. V. Nemytskii and V. V. Stepanov, *Qualitative Theory of
  Differential Equations***. The classical treatise on qualitative theory,
  stability, and abstract dynamical systems.
* **Lamberto Cesari, *Asymptotic Behavior and Stability Problems in
  Ordinary Differential Equations***. The classical survey of stability,
  asymptotics, and periodic solutions.
* **Carl Ludwig Siegel and Jürgen K. Moser, *Lectures on Celestial
  Mechanics***. The three-body problem, periodic solutions, and small
  divisors, by two founders of KAM theory.
* **Stephen Wiggins, *Normally Hyperbolic Invariant Manifolds in Dynamical
  Systems***. Fenichel's theory of persistence of invariant manifolds.
* **Jack K. Hale, Luis T. Magalhães, and Waldyr M. Oliva, *Dynamics in Infinite
  Dimensions*** (2nd ed., 2002). Invariant manifolds and attractors for delay equations and
  dissipative partial differential equations, with an appendix by Krzysztof P. Rybakowski.
  Distinct from Hale's *Ordinary Differential Equations*.
* **Peter Constantin, Ciprian Foias, Basil Nicolaenko, and Roger Temam, *Integral Manifolds
  and Inertial Manifolds for Dissipative Partial Differential Equations***. Inertial
  manifolds and finite-dimensional reduction for dissipative evolution equations. Distinct
  from Constantin and Foias, *Navier–Stokes Equations*.
* **Stephen Wiggins, *Global Bifurcations and Chaos: Analytical Methods***. Global
  bifurcations, homoclinic orbits, and analytic methods for detecting chaos. Distinct from
  his *Introduction to Applied Nonlinear Dynamical Systems and Chaos* and from *Normally
  Hyperbolic Invariant Manifolds in Dynamical Systems*.
* **Kenneth R. Meyer and Daniel C. Offin, *Introduction to Hamiltonian Dynamical Systems and
  the N-Body Problem*** (3rd ed., 2017,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-53691-0)). Hamiltonian systems, stability,
  and the N-body problem. The first edition, with Glen R. Hall, appeared in 1992.
* **Edson de Faria and Welington de Melo, *Mathematical Tools for One-Dimensional
  Dynamics***. Quasiconformal mappings, Riemann surfaces, and the analytic tools of
  one-dimensional dynamics. Distinct from Palis and de Melo, *Geometric Theory of Dynamical
  Systems*.

### More recent works

* **Gerald Teschl, *Ordinary Differential Equations and Dynamical
  Systems*** (2012, American Mathematical Society). A modern rigorous
  graduate course connecting ODE, dynamical systems, and spectral
  theory; freely available.
* **Luis Barreira and Claudia Valls, *Ordinary Differential Equations:
  Qualitative Theory*** (2012, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/137), Graduate
  Studies in Mathematics). A modern qualitative ODE course: conjugacies,
  centre manifolds, and normal forms.
* **David G. Schaeffer and John W. Cain, *Ordinary Differential Equations:
  Basics and Beyond*** (2016,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4939-6389-8)). A broad graduate
  course combining classical ODE theory with bifurcations and dynamical
  systems. Strong on examples that explain the qualitative theory.
* **Thomas C. Sideris, *Ordinary Differential Equations and Dynamical
  Systems*** (2013, [Atlantis
  Press](https://doi.org/10.2991/978-94-6239-021-8)). A concise graduate
  introduction to existence, stability, phase-space geometry, and dynamical
  methods. Useful as preparation for PDE evolution and hyperbolic dynamics.
* **Henk Broer and Floris Takens, *Dynamical Systems and Chaos*** (2011,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4419-6870-8)). Smooth dynamics,
  bifurcations, hyperbolicity, and chaotic behaviour in a compact graduate
  treatment. Offers a bridge from ODE courses to the structural theory.
* **Lan Wen, *Differentiable Dynamical Systems: An Introduction to
  Structural Stability and Hyperbolicity*** (2016, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/173), Graduate Studies in Mathematics). Structural stability and
  hyperbolicity, in a self-contained graduate account.
* **Christian Kuehn, *Multiple Time Scale Dynamics*** (2015,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-12316-5)). Slow–fast systems,
  geometric singular perturbation, bifurcations, and stochastic extensions. A
  substantial graduate-to-research synthesis of methods scattered across the
  perturbation literature.
* **Mariana Haragus and Gérard Iooss, *Local Bifurcations, Center Manifolds,
  and Normal Forms in Infinite-Dimensional Dynamical Systems*** (2011,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-85729-112-7), Universitext).
  Center manifolds and normal forms for evolution equations and for spatial
  dynamics, with water waves and pattern formation as applications.
* **Peter E. Kloeden and Martin Rasmussen, *Nonautonomous Dynamical
  Systems*** (2011, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/176), Mathematical Surveys and
  Monographs). Processes, skew products, pullback attraction, and
  nonautonomous bifurcation.
* **Igor Chueshov, *Dynamics of Quasi-Stable Dissipative Systems*** (2015,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-22903-4)). Quasi-stability,
  attractors, and long-time behaviour of nonlinear evolution equations. A
  specialised continuation of infinite-dimensional dynamical-systems theory.
* **Dmitry Treschev and Oleg Zubelevich, *Introduction to the
  Perturbation Theory of Hamiltonian Systems*** (2010, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-03028-4)).
  Averaging, KAM, and separatrix splitting in a short course.
* **Alfonso Sorrentino, *Action-Minimizing Methods in Hamiltonian
  Dynamics: An Introduction to Aubry–Mather Theory*** (2015, [Princeton
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1515/9781400866618), Annals of Mathematics Studies). Aubry–Mather theory
  and weak KAM, as a graduate monograph. Albert Fathi's long-circulated
  lecture notes remain the other standard entry to weak KAM.
* **Vadim Kaloshin and Ke Zhang, *Arnold Diffusion for Smooth Systems of
  Two and a Half Degrees of Freedom*** (2020, [Princeton University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1515/9780691204932), Annals of Mathematics Studies).
  Arnold diffusion for nearly integrable Hamiltonian systems with two and a
  half degrees of freedom, including the construction of orbits with large
  drift.
* **Shangjiang Guo and Jianhong Wu, *Bifurcation Theory of Functional
  Differential Equations*** (2013, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-6992-6)). Normal forms and
  bifurcations for delay equations.
* **Todd Fisher and Boris Hasselblatt, *Hyperbolic Flows*** (2019,
  [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/200)). Listed in full in Section 10.
* **José F. Alves, *Nonuniformly Hyperbolic Attractors*** (2020,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-62814-7)). Listed in full in Section 10.
* **Marcelo Viana, *Lectures on Lyapunov Exponents*** (2014, [Cambridge
  University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139976602)). Listed in full in Section 10.
* **Anatole Katok and Viorel Niţică, *Rigidity in Higher Rank Abelian
  Group Actions***, volume I (2011, [Cambridge University Press](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511803550)). Listed in full in Section 10.
* **Àlex Haro, Marta Canadell, Jordi-Lluís Figueras, Alejandro Luque, and Josep-Maria
  Mondelo, *The Parameterization Method for Invariant Manifolds: From Rigorous Results to
  Effective Computations*** (2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-29662-3)).
  The parameterization method for invariant manifolds, from the theorems to validated
  computation.

---

<a id="ckma-32"></a>

## 32. Partial Differential Equations: General and Linear Theory

General PDE courses and linear differential and boundary-value theory
provide a common framework of distributions, weak solutions, energy methods,
ellipticity, and well-posedness. The section includes broad courses
extending beyond linear equations, as well as specialised linear and
higher-order treatments. Section 30 develops microlocal methods, Section 33
nonlinear regularity and free boundaries, and Section 34 hyperbolic,
dispersive, fluid, and kinetic equations. Abstract evolution and integral
formulations are treated in Sections 24–25.

### Foundational works

* **Lawrence C. Evans, *Partial Differential Equations***. The
  standard first graduate course: characteristics, the classical
  equations, Sobolev spaces, weak solutions, elliptic regularity, and
  variational methods.
* **Michael E. Taylor, *Partial Differential Equations
  I–III***. Broader and more geometric, extending to spectral theory,
  microlocal analysis, and nonlinear problems.
  Also listed in Section 1.
* **Gerald B. Folland, *Introduction to Partial Differential
  Equations***. A compact analytic course centred on distributions,
  the Fourier transform, and constant-coefficient equations.
* **Michael Renardy and Robert C. Rogers, *An Introduction to Partial
  Differential Equations***. A balanced graduate text covering
  functional analysis, distributions, elliptic theory, and evolution
  equations.
* **Jeffrey Rauch, *Partial Differential Equations***. Strong on
  hyperbolic equations, energy estimates, and propagation.
* **Vladimir I. Arnold, *Lectures on Partial Differential Equations***.
  Short and geometric; the classical equations seen through
  characteristics, symmetry, and physical intuition.
* **Fritz John, *Partial Differential Equations***. Short, geometric,
  and influential.
* **Lipman Bers, Fritz John, and Martin Schechter, *Partial Differential
  Equations***. A classical Courant Institute text with unusually
  broad coverage.
* **François Trèves, *Basic Linear Partial Differential
  Equations***. Linear PDE through distributions and Fourier methods.
* **Friedrich Sauvigny, *Partial Differential Equations*** (2 volumes, 2nd
  ed., 2012: *Foundations and Integral Representations*; *Functional Analytic
  Methods*, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4471-2981-3); [volume II](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4471-2984-4)). A
  substantial course developing classical representations and
  functional-analytic methods together. Useful for readers wanting the
  potential-theoretic and operator approaches in one sequence.
* **Lars Hörmander, *The Analysis of Linear Partial Differential
  Operators I–IV***. A four-volume research reference for distributions,
  linear differential operators, pseudodifferential calculus, and
  propagation of singularities.
  Also listed in Section 1.
* **S. Mizohata, *The Theory of Partial Differential Equations***. A
  classical Japanese-school treatment strong on energy methods and
  well-posedness.
* **Vladimir A. Kozlov, Vladimir G. Maz'ya, and Jürgen Rossmann, *Elliptic
  Boundary Value Problems in Domains with Point Singularities***. Elliptic
  boundary-value problems near conical and cuspidal points, using operator
  pencils and asymptotic expansions of solutions.
* **Pierre Grisvard, *Elliptic Problems in Nonsmooth Domains***. The
  standard source for regularity on polygonal and Lipschitz domains.
* **J.-L. Lions and E. Magenes, *Non-Homogeneous Boundary Value
  Problems and Applications*** (3 volumes). The functional-analytic
  theory of boundary value problems and evolution equations.
* **Robert C. McOwen, *Partial Differential Equations: Methods and
  Applications***. A first graduate course emphasizing methods and
  applications.
* **Emmanuele DiBenedetto, *Partial Differential Equations***. A
  rigorous first course with a more classical emphasis.
* **Sandro Salsa, *Partial Differential Equations in
  Action***. Modern, applied-facing, and good for modelling context.
* **Peter J. Olver, *Applications of Lie Groups to Differential
  Equations***. Symmetry groups, conservation laws, and Noether's theorem
  for differential equations.
* **Jindřich Nečas, *Direct Methods in the Theory of Elliptic
  Equations***. The classical treatment of variational methods for elliptic
  boundary-value problems in Sobolev spaces.
* **Demetrios Christodoulou, *The Action Principle and Partial Differential Equations***.
  The calculus of variations for systems of partial differential equations, and the action
  principle as a source of hyperbolic equations. Distinct from his monographs on the
  Einstein equations.
* **Olga A. Ladyzhenskaya, *The Boundary Value Problems of Mathematical Physics***.
  Functional-analytic methods for elliptic, parabolic, and hyperbolic boundary-value
  problems. Distinct from Ladyzhenskaya and Ural'tseva and from Ladyzhenskaya, Solonnikov,
  and Ural'tseva.
* **Olle Stormark, *Lie's Structural Approach to PDE Systems***. Lie's theory of local
  solvability and the structure of overdetermined systems of partial differential equations.

### More recent works

* **Qing Han, *A Basic Course in Partial Differential Equations***
  (2011, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/120), Graduate Studies in Mathematics).
  A first graduate course organised by method rather than by equation.
* **Alberto Bressan, *Lecture Notes on Functional Analysis: With Applications
  to Linear Partial Differential Equations*** (2013, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://www.ams.org/books/gsm/143/)). Functional analysis, Sobolev
  spaces, and semigroups developed toward elliptic, parabolic, and hyperbolic
  equations. A concise graduate bridge between the abstract theory and PDE.
* **Dorina Mitrea, *Distributions, Partial Differential Equations, and
  Harmonic Analysis*** (2nd ed., 2018, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-03296-8)). Listed in full in Section 16.
* **Luigi Ambrosio, Alessandro Carlotto, and Annalisa Massaccesi,
  *Lectures on Elliptic Partial Differential Equations*** (2018,
  [Edizioni della Normale](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-88-7642-651-3)). A short modern course from Laplace's equation
  to De Giorgi–Nash–Moser theory.
  Also listed in Section 33.
* **Xavier Fernández-Real and Xavier Ros-Oton, *Regularity Theory for
  Elliptic PDE*** (2022, [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/zlam/28)).
  Listed in full in Section 33. A second course on elliptic regularity,
  including Schauder estimates and nonlinear equations.
* **Filippo Gazzola, Hans-Christoph Grunau, and Guido Sweers,
  *Polyharmonic Boundary Value Problems*** (2010, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-12245-3), Lecture
  Notes in Mathematics). Higher-order elliptic equations, positivity,
  and the failure of the maximum principle.
* **Vladimir G. Maz'ya, *Boundary Behavior of Solutions to Elliptic Equations
  in General Domains*** (2018, [European Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/190)). Wiener-type criteria, boundary
  regularity, and capacity methods for elliptic equations in irregular
  domains.

---

<a id="ckma-33"></a>

## 33. Elliptic and Parabolic Equations: Regularity, Free Boundaries, and Viscosity Solutions

Elliptic and parabolic equations are studied through weak and viscosity
solutions, interior and boundary regularity, fully nonlinear and degenerate
equations, nonlinear diffusion, and free boundaries. Hamilton–Jacobi
equations and related control and mean-field-game problems are included
through their comparison and regularity methods. Section 32 supplies general
linear theory, Section 35 abstract existence methods, Section 36 variational
constructions, and Section 39C transport-related Monge–Ampère theory.
Hyperbolic and dispersive evolution is organised in Section 34.

### Foundational works

* **David Gilbarg and Neil S. Trudinger, *Elliptic Partial
  Differential Equations of Second Order***. The central reference:
  Schauder estimates, maximum principles, De Giorgi–Nash–Moser theory,
  and quasilinear equations.
* **Qing Han and Fanghua Lin, *Elliptic Partial Differential
  Equations***. A concise second course emphasizing modern regularity
  arguments.
* **Olga A. Ladyzhenskaya and Nina N. Ural'tseva, *Linear and
  Quasilinear Elliptic Equations***. The foundational source of the
  Russian regularity theory.
* **Olga A. Ladyzhenskaya, Vsevolod A. Solonnikov, and Nina N. Ural'tseva,
  *Linear and Quasilinear Equations of Parabolic Type***. The
  classical large reference for parabolic estimates.
* **Avner Friedman, *Partial Differential Equations of Parabolic
  Type***. A classical systematic account.
* **Gary M. Lieberman, *Second Order Parabolic Differential
  Equations***. A modern regularity reference, especially strong on
  boundary estimates.
* **N. V. Krylov, *Lectures on Elliptic and Parabolic Equations in
  Hölder Spaces*** and ***…in Sobolev Spaces***. Schauder and Sobolev
  theory from the Krylov school, with the fully nonlinear techniques
  included.
* **N. V. Krylov, *Nonlinear Elliptic and Parabolic Equations of the
  Second Order***. The Krylov–Safonov theory and fully nonlinear
  regularity.
* **Luis A. Caffarelli and Xavier Cabré, *Fully Nonlinear Elliptic
  Equations***. The standard short monograph on viscosity solutions
  and nonlinear elliptic regularity.
* **Mariano Giaquinta, *Multiple Integrals in the Calculus of
  Variations and Nonlinear Elliptic Systems***. The reference for
  partial regularity of systems and Morrey/Campanato technique.
* **Mariano Giaquinta and Luca Martinazzi, *An Introduction to the
  Regularity Theory for Elliptic Systems, Harmonic Maps and Minimal
  Graphs***. A modern, more readable route to the same material.
* **Enrico Giusti, *Direct Methods in the Calculus of
  Variations***. Regularity for minimizers, with the De Giorgi
  technique developed carefully.
  Also listed in Section 36.
* **Michael G. Crandall, Hitoshi Ishii, and Pierre-Louis Lions,
  *User's Guide to Viscosity Solutions of Second Order Partial Differential
  Equations*** (1992, survey article in *Bulletin of the American
  Mathematical Society* 27(1), pp. 1–67,
  [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/S0273-0979-1992-00266-5)).
  A survey of viscosity solutions, including comparison principles,
  existence, stability, and the treatment of boundary conditions for
  second-order PDE.
* **Guy Barles, *Solutions de viscosité des équations de
  Hamilton–Jacobi***. The standard monograph on viscosity solutions
  for first-order equations.
* **Martino Bardi and Italo Capuzzo-Dolcetta, *Optimal Control and
  Viscosity Solutions of Hamilton–Jacobi–Bellman Equations***. The
  control-theoretic treatment.
* **Shigeaki Koike, *A Beginner's Guide to the Theory of Viscosity
  Solutions***. Short and genuinely introductory.
* **Piermarco Cannarsa and Carlo Sinestrari, *Semiconcave Functions,
  Hamilton–Jacobi Equations, and Optimal Control*** (2004,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/b138356)). Semiconcavity, the
  singularities of viscosity solutions, and their propagation.
* **Luis A. Caffarelli and Sandro Salsa, *A Geometric Approach to Free
  Boundary Problems***. The modern entry to free-boundary regularity.
* **David Kinderlehrer and Guido Stampacchia, *An Introduction to
  Variational Inequalities and Their Applications***. The foundational
  book for obstacle problems and variational inequalities.
* **Avner Friedman, *Variational Principles and Free-Boundary
  Problems***. A classical bridge between variational methods and free
  boundaries.
* **Cristian E. Gutiérrez, *The Monge–Ampère Equation***. The standard
  earlier treatment of Aleksandrov solutions and Caffarelli's
  regularity theory.
* **Peter Lindqvist, *Notes on the p-Laplace Equation***. A concise
  introduction to the p-Laplace equation.
* **Juha Heinonen, Tero Kilpeläinen, and Olli Martio, *Nonlinear
  Potential Theory of Degenerate Elliptic Equations***. The standard
  monograph on degenerate elliptic equations.
  Also listed in Section 38.
* **Juan Luis Vázquez, *The Porous Medium Equation: Mathematical Theory***
  (2007, [Oxford University Press](https://doi.org/10.1093/acprof:oso/9780198569039.001.0001)). The monograph on the porous-medium
  equation: existence, regularity, and asymptotics.
* **Juan Luis Vázquez, *Smoothing and Decay Estimates for Nonlinear
  Diffusion Equations: Equations of Porous Medium Type*** (2006, [Oxford
  University
  Press](https://doi.org/10.1093/acprof:oso/9780199202973.001.0001)). Fast
  diffusion and porous medium equations: smoothing effects, extinction, and
  asymptotics. The companion to *The Porous Medium Equation*.
* **Emmanuele DiBenedetto, *Degenerate Parabolic Equations***. The
  monograph on degenerate and singular parabolic equations, intrinsic
  scaling, and the Harnack inequality. The later accounts of intrinsic
  scaling rest on it. Distinct from his *Real Analysis* and *Partial
  Differential Equations*.
* **José Miguel Urbano, *The Method of Intrinsic Scaling: A Systematic
  Approach to Regularity for Degenerate and Singular PDEs*** (2008,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-75932-4), Lecture Notes in Mathematics). Intrinsic scaling as a
  method, in a short research course.
* **Pavol Quittner and Philippe Souplet, *Superlinear Parabolic Problems:
  Blow-up, Global Existence and Steady States*** (2nd ed., 2019,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-18222-9)). A substantial
  reference for reaction–diffusion equations, blow-up, and global behaviour.
  Combines regularity and comparison methods with long-time dynamics.
* **Andrea Bonfiglioli, Ermanno Lanconelli, and Francesco Uguzzoni,
  *Stratified Lie Groups and Potential Theory for Their
  Sub-Laplacians***. Subelliptic equations in the group setting.
* **Murray H. Protter and Hans F. Weinberger, *Maximum Principles in
  Differential Equations***. The classical account of a technique used
  everywhere in this section.
* **Kazuaki Taira, *Boundary Value Problems and Markov Processes*** (2nd ed., 2009).
  Elliptic boundary-value problems and the Markov processes generated by their operators.

### More recent works

* **Xavier Fernández-Real and Xavier Ros-Oton, *Regularity Theory for
  Elliptic PDE*** (2022, [European Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.4171/zlam/28), Zurich Lectures
  in Advanced Mathematics). A self-contained regularity course:
  Schauder estimates, Hilbert's nineteenth problem, fully nonlinear
  equations, and the obstacle problem.
  Also listed in Section 32.
* **Luigi Ambrosio, Alessandro Carlotto, and Annalisa Massaccesi,
  *Lectures on Elliptic Partial Differential Equations*** (2018,
  [Edizioni della Normale](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-88-7642-651-3)). Listed in full in Section 32.
* **Marino Badiale and Enrico Serra, *Semilinear Elliptic Equations for
  Beginners: Existence Results via the Variational Approach*** (2011,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-85729-227-8)). Variational
  construction of semilinear elliptic solutions, including critical-point
  methods. A graduate bridge from functional analysis to nonlinear elliptic
  existence theory.
* **Hervé Le Dret, *Nonlinear Elliptic Partial Differential Equations: An
  Introduction*** (2018; English translation,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-78390-1)). Weak formulations,
  monotonicity, and variational methods for nonlinear elliptic problems. A
  course-length introduction before the specialised regularity monographs.
* **Qing Han, *Nonlinear Elliptic Equations of the Second Order*** (2016,
  [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/171), Graduate
  Studies in Mathematics). Quasilinear and fully nonlinear equations,
  including the minimal surface and Monge–Ampère equations, with the a
  priori estimates done carefully.
* **N. V. Krylov, *Sobolev and Viscosity Solutions for Fully Nonlinear
  Elliptic and Parabolic Equations*** (2018, [American Mathematical
  Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/233), Mathematical Surveys and
  Monographs). W^{2,p} solvability and viscosity solutions for fully
  nonlinear equations, by one of the founders of the regularity theory.
* **Alessio Figalli, *The Monge–Ampère Equation and Its
  Applications*** (2017, European Mathematical Society). Regularity
  theory for Monge–Ampère, with the optimal-transport connection.
  Also listed in Section 39.
* **Nam Q. Le, *Analysis of Monge–Ampère Equations*** (2024, [American
  Mathematical
  Society](https://www.ams.org/books/gsm/240/gsm240-endmatter.pdf)). Interior
  and boundary regularity, the linearized equation, and geometric
  applications. A substantial later synthesis extending the graduate
  treatments of Gutiérrez and Figalli listed in this section.
* **Arshak Petrosyan, Henrik Shahgholian, and Nina N. Ural'tseva,
  *Regularity of Free Boundaries in Obstacle-Type Problems*** (2012,
  American Mathematical Society, Graduate Studies in Mathematics). A
  graduate text on obstacle-type free-boundary problems that reaches the
  research literature.
* **Bozhidar Velichkov, *Regularity of the One-phase Free Boundaries***
  (2023, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-13238-4), Lecture Notes of the Unione Matematica Italiana). An
  open-access introduction to one-phase free-boundary regularity,
  organised around four theorems.
  Also listed in Sections 14 and 37.
* **Xavier Fernández-Real and Xavier Ros-Oton, *Integro-Differential
  Elliptic Equations*** (2024,
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-54242-8), Progress in
  Mathematics). Regularity for the fractional Laplacian and general nonlocal
  operators, including fully nonlinear and obstacle problems. The nonlocal
  sequel to their *Regularity Theory for Elliptic PDE*.
* **Claudia Bucur and Enrico Valdinoci, *Nonlocal Diffusion and
  Applications*** (2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-28739-3)). Listed in full in Section 26.
* **Louis Dupaigne, *Stable Solutions of Elliptic Partial Differential
  Equations*** (2011, [CRC Press](https://doi.org/10.1201/b10802)). Stable solutions, extremal solutions,
  and the regularity theory around them.
* **Emmanuele DiBenedetto, Ugo Gianazza, and Vincenzo Vespri,
  *Harnack's Inequality for Degenerate and Singular Parabolic
  Equations*** (2012, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4614-1584-8)). Intrinsic scaling and Harnack
  inequalities for degenerate parabolic equations.
* **Ansgar Jüngel, *Entropy Methods for Diffusive Partial Differential
  Equations*** (2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-34219-1)).
  Entropy dissipation, existence, and convergence to equilibrium for diffusion
  systems. A short introduction to estimates particularly useful when maximum
  principles are unavailable.
* **Vitaly Volpert, *Elliptic Partial Differential Equations*** (2 volumes:
  *Fredholm Theory of Elliptic Problems in Unbounded Domains* (2011);
  *Reaction-Diffusion Equations* (2014),
  [Birkhäuser](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-0346-0537-3); [volume II](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-0348-0813-2)). Elliptic problems
  on unbounded domains, Fredholm properties, and reaction–diffusion models. A
  research treatise complementing bounded-domain regularity books.
* **Hung Vinh Tran, *Hamilton–Jacobi Equations: Theory and Applications***
  (2021, [American Mathematical Society](https://doi.org/10.1090/gsm/213),
  Graduate Studies in Mathematics). Viscosity solutions, optimal control,
  weak KAM theory, and periodic homogenization of Hamilton–Jacobi equations.
* **Marta Lewicka, *A Course on Tug-of-War Games with Random Noise:
  Introduction and Basic Constructions*** (2020,
  [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-46209-3), Universitext). The
  game-theoretic approach to the p-Laplacian and the infinity Laplacian.
* **Brian Street, *Maximal Subellipticity*** (2023, [De Gruyter](https://doi.org/10.1515/9783111085647), Studies
  in Mathematics). Interior regularity for maximally subelliptic
  equations, linear and fully nonlinear, with the adapted function
  spaces.
  Also listed in Sections 27 and 30.
* **Diogo A. Gomes, Edgard A. Pimentel, and Vardan Voskanyan,
  *Regularity Theory for Mean-Field Game Systems*** (2016, [Springer](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-38934-9)).
  A short account of the elliptic and parabolic regularity of
  mean-field game systems.
